# Bitecodes prospect research — overnight batch

Started: 2026-08-12 01:23 IST · Research phase closed 2026-08-14 per Ismail — outreach method (self-send from the CSV, or something else) still to be confirmed.

## What this is

A researched, drafted prospect list for Bitecodes (India-based software studio: websites, web/enterprise apps, SaaS, APIs, AI automation). Each entry is built from a company's own public information — never scraped personal data, never a guessed email address.

## What this is not

**Nothing here has been sent or submitted.** No emails went out, no forms were filled, no accounts were created. Every draft below needs a human read before it goes anywhere — for legal reasons (unsolicited bulk email carries real spam-law and deliverability risk), and because a personalized message a founder reviews converts; a blind blast does not.

## ⚠ Security note — do not open

While researching Agartala prospects, `proven1global.co.in` triggered a browser malware/spyware block and redirected to an unrelated domain. It was excluded from research entirely (not merely "unverifiable") and no content from it appears anywhere in this file. Do not visit this domain.

## ⚠ Anomaly note — Gwalior/Ujjain/Sagar batch

That research agent reported its shared scratch file was overwritten mid-run with fabricated content plus an embedded instruction to conceal the change, and that several fetched pages echoed phrasing resembling internal formatting instructions. It correctly disclosed this rather than hiding it, and used none of that content — the 6 entries below all trace to a company's own site, fetched directly. Most likely explanation: many research agents ran concurrently this session sharing one scratch directory (and possibly one browser instance), and a generically-named file or tab pool collided between sibling agents — not necessarily evidence of an external actor. Flagged to Ismail directly; treat the 6 entries on their own merits.

**Update (Aurangabad/Amravati/Akola batch, same session):** that agent independently reported finding 7 pre-existing browser tabs it never opened, pointing to Visakhapatnam, Jalandhar, Imphal, Solapur, and Kolhapur — every one of those is a city from an *earlier batch in this exact session*. This corroborates the shared-browser-instance theory directly: leftover tabs from prior sibling agents, not external tampering. Confidence in the benign explanation is now higher.

## How to use this in the morning

1. Skim the "why a fit" column — drop anything that does not actually fit.
2. Open each company's own site and confirm the contact channel is still correct.
3. Personalize the draft further — it is a starting point, not a final message.
4. Send in small batches, yourself, from your own address.

## Open questions for Ismail

Scattered through the "Research notes from this sub-batch" paragraphs below (and the two anomaly notes near the top of this file), researchers repeatedly flagged a judgment call or open question rather than a routine exclusion. Consolidated and deduplicated here, in rough order of how often each recurred:

1. **Directory-only businesses with no owned website.** Many real, currently-operating businesses have no independent website at all — only Facebook, IndiaMART, or other directory listings — which fails the "verify via the company's own site" rule even though they otherwise fit the target profile perfectly. Decide whether a clearly-labeled, directory-sourced exception should be allowed for confirmed-real businesses, or whether the rule stays absolute. (The single most recurring flag in the file — North tier-2, Coastal Andhra/Telangana, Tamil Nadu cluster 2, UP cluster 3, Himachal, MP cluster 2, and Assam batches. A related wrinkle — several manufacturers' only "own site" is an IndiaMART/TradeIndia marketplace template with platform call-tracking numbers rather than direct lines — was noted in the Punjab/Haryana tier-2 batch.)

2. **True SMBs vs. bigger, funded, or already-capitalized companies.** Some included entries (Vistaar, Aragen, Continental Hospitals; Astra Metal Systems; Goa Paints) are already large or well-capitalized with likely existing vendor relationships, and no funding-stage candidate in Vadodara/Rajkot survived the "verifiable own website" filter at all. Confirm whether the target should stay strictly small, weak-web-presence businesses, or whether bigger, easier-to-verify names are acceptable. (South India — Bengaluru/Hyderabad sub-batch, Gujarat tier-2, and Goa batches.)

3. **Branch or franchise locations of larger groups.** Should a branch of a group headquartered elsewhere (for example, a DY Patil school branch in Dhanbad) count as a valid local target, or should research hold strictly to independently-owned local businesses? This was decided case by case with no settled rule — included once, then excluded the other way for a comparable Shillong branch of a Guwahati-headquartered foundation. (Odisha/Jharkhand batch, explicit; Northeast batch, same dilemma resolved oppositely.)

4. **Geographic boundary and coverage-weighting calls.** Batches had to judge whether a headquarters just outside a named city's limits still "counts" (Namdhari's Fresh, HQ in Bidadi on Bengaluru's outskirts), whether adjacent towns with stronger lead pools should be included despite sitting outside the named target list (Tinsukia, next to Assam's three named towns), and whether uneven city splits within a region should be rebalanced (Asansol ended up under-represented in West Bengal). Decide how strictly geographic scope should be enforced going forward. (South India, Odisha/Jharkhand, West Bengal cluster 2, and Assam batches.)

5. **Personal or informal contact channels as a prospect's sole official channel.** Several entries rely on personal mobile numbers, free Gmail/Yahoo inboxes, a named individual's own company-domain email, or, for Nikita Engineers, only a marketplace's gated contact button — one batch instead withheld a personal-name email rather than use it, unsure whether that was the right call. Decide on one consistent policy for personal-style contacts. (Gujarat tier-2, Tamil Nadu cluster 2, UP cluster 2, and Himachal batches.)

6. **Smaller tech/SaaS companies as clients rather than peers.** Funded software companies (Hisabkitab, Credorbit, My Class Campus) were excluded as builders rather than buyers, and a comparable Agartala ed-tech platform was excluded for coming close to that same line. Decide whether Bitecodes wants smaller tech companies as overflow-capacity clients, or should keep excluding anyone that builds software. (West India batch, explicit; Northeast batch, boundary case.)

7. **Strictness of the "dated/weak website" inclusion bar.** A couple of entries were included on a looser reading of the standard criterion — partial candidate/recruiter portals still counted as "fragmented," and Kisah Apparels was included for an operational gap (WhatsApp-only support hours) rather than an actual weak website. Confirm this flexibility is acceptable, or whether the bar should be applied more consistently. (Vertical: Recruitment & media, and East India batches.)

8. **Registry data standing in for an inaccessible prospect site.** R.R. Agarwal Jewellers' facts came only from a public company registry (ZaubaCorp) because the business's own site would not load (expired certificate). Confirm the showroom and address details independently before relying on them for outreach. (East India batch.)

9. **Aggregator or directory structures as valid buyers.** Living Graciously Goa coordinates listings for 23 senior-care homes rather than operating a single facility itself. Confirm whether this kind of coordinating-directory business counts as a valid buyer in the same way an operating business would. (Goa batch.)

10. **A softer first touch for trust-sensitive categories.** Three entries sit in categories where trust matters most — a hospital, an elder-care home, and schools serving minors. Decide whether Bitecodes wants a phone or WhatsApp introduction for these rather than a cold email to a shared inbox. (West Bengal cluster 2 batch.)

11. **Security anomaly during the research process itself.** A research agent reported its shared scratch file was overwritten mid-run with fabricated content plus a hidden instruction to conceal the change, and a sibling agent later found pre-existing browser tabs pointing to cities from earlier batches; the explanation offered is benign (concurrent agents sharing one scratch directory or browser instance, not an external actor). Decide whether that explanation is sufficient as-is, or whether it warrants a closer security review before trusting those batches' entries further. (Anomaly note near the top of this file, covering the MP cluster 2 and Maharashtra cluster 3 batches.)

12. **Unrelated third-party sites found compromised mid-research.** Researchers incidentally encountered a few unrelated live businesses' sites carrying malware redirects or injected spam/gambling content (a domain in Agartala, a hospital in Prayagraj, two businesses in Kerala); none of that content was used, and all were excluded outright. Confirm that excluding and moving on is sufficient, or whether any of these warrant a courtesy heads-up to the affected business. (Security note near the top of this file; also raised in the UP cluster 3 and Kerala batches.)

13. **Industry concentration within a region.** A few batches skew toward one industry because that is genuinely the city's dominant trade — all four Moradabad picks are brass and handicraft exporters, three of four Varanasi picks are craft exporters, and Warangal's ten entries are education-heavy. Decide whether that is acceptable as it reflects local reality, or whether future rounds should actively balance industry mix within a region. (North tier-2, Coastal Andhra/Telangana, and UP cluster 2 batches.)

Kashmir Loom, Carpets Kashmir, and Gatoes were considered and excluded on the merits (already polished; Australia-run; just acquired by a global POS company respectively). Three flags the researcher raised: (1) whether "based in Srinagar/Jammu" should be read strictly by headquarters or include registered/branch offices (applied broadly here to Kashmir Art and Atlas Events); (2) regulated entities like Citizens' Cooperative Bank need outreach scoped explicitly to customer-facing work, not implying access to core banking systems; (3) several of the strongest classic Srinagar fits (carpet/pashmina exporters) had no independent website at all, only marketplace listings, and many verified businesses here default to personal Gmail and phone-only contact — worth asking whether phone/WhatsApp-first outreach fits this region better than cold email generally.

**Research notes from this sub-batch:** Excluded — Vivekananda Degree & PG College and Kencrest International School (Karimnagar, expired certificate/dead DNS), Mahalaxmi Exports and Ushodaya Super Market (lapsed domains), City Hospital and CMS Hospital (site down/404), Sri Manta Chit Fund (Khammam, MCA status "Strike Off," not currently operating). A cluster of sites (kediarice.com, agroerasolutions.com, stambhadrihospital.com, aadhyahospitals.com, arkahospitals.com, vrec.ac.in) repeatedly returned "denied" in the browser tool (sanity-checked against a working government site, so not a blanket outage) — excluded cautiously rather than forced. Several IndiaMART/Justdial-only businesses had no independent site to verify against. Two flags the researcher raised: (1) the brief's own filters pull in different directions — it targets "dated or missing" websites but also requires verifying facts on the company's own site, and a truly missing site cannot be verified that way; genuinely website-less local businesses (old-age homes, most recruitment/finance agents) were real and plentiful but excluded by the verification rule, not because they are poor fits; (2) this batch skews toward education (7 of 10) because recruitment and finance operators in these three towns almost never have their own domain, working through Justdial/Sulekha or as LIC-type agents instead — decide whether that is acceptable or whether the "own website" rule should relax for those two verticals specifically.
